I'm Dr. Smith. To get us started, could you tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I suppose... well, things have just gotten very quiet. I used to reach out to people, you know? And I enjoyed things. My work, going to yoga, looking at galleries... even just writing in my journal. But now... I'm mostly just going through the motions. Fulfilling obligations. That's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period like this before, where you felt this way for an extended time, or is this the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: I don't... I'm not really sure. I don't think it's ever been quite like this before. Not for this long, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Did anything specific happen in your life, such as a major change or a difficult event, around the time you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been about eight months now. We... we took an older relative into our apartment around then. I guess that's when this whole change started. Just... how I'm thinking, and working. Connecting with people. It's been going on since they moved in.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your sleep patterns, such as difficulty falling asleep, staying asleep, or sleeping too much, since this started?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. My sleep has been fine, I suppose. Just... normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty with your ability to focus, concentrate, or make decisions since this started?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't really noticed any problems with that. I can concentrate fine, I think.\n\nDoctor: Since this started eight months ago, would you say your mood has been consistently low most of the day, nearly every day, or does it tend to lift when you are away from your home environment?\n\nPatient: I don't... I wouldn't really say my mood has been low. It's just... I don't know. I feel fine, mostly. It doesn't really change if I leave the apartment. It's not a mood thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you feel uncomfortable or helpless when you are alone because you fear you cannot take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: No... I don't feel helpless. I take care of myself just fine. Being alone isn't... it's not a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: When you say you are just 'going through the motions' and have lost interest in things you used to enjoy, do you feel more of an underlying sense of worry or nervousness, or do you feel a sense of sadness or tearfulness?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really say it's either of those. I don't... I'm not crying or feeling sad. And I don't feel nervous, really. It's just... I don't know. I just don't feel much of anything right now. I still show up to work. I'm there. But... I guess things have slipped a little bit. I missed a few grant deadlines recently. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: When you say you don't feel much of anything, do you feel that this lack of interest and engagement is specifically a reaction to the stress and changes brought on by having your relative move in with you?\n\nPatient: I suppose so... yeah. It's been going on for about eight months now.\n\nDoctor: Since your relative moved in, have you noticed yourself acting out or behaving in ways that are out of character for you, such as being more irritable, impulsive, or prone to conflict?\n\nPatient: I don't... I wouldn't say I'm irritable or getting into conflicts. That's not it. It's more... just odd things. Like... a few times in team meetings, or even when I'm sitting with a client, I'll realize I'm lining up all the pens on the desk in these very specific, exact patterns. Or... someone will say something, and I'll catch myself repeating their exact phrase out loud before I can stop it. I don't mean to. But... people notice. They look at me like I'm... like I'm not really there, or something.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any new physical symptoms, such as headaches, vision changes, or any unusual sensations in your body, since this started eight months ago?\n\nPatient: No... no, I haven't noticed anything like that. I haven't had any tests like that. Just... no.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your social behavior, such as saying things that are socially inappropriate or acting in a way that feels uninhibited compared to how you used to behave?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. My sleep has been fine, I suppose. I haven't really noticed any problems with that. I can concentrate fine, I think.\n\nDoctor: Since this started eight months ago, would you say your mood has been consistently low most of the day, nearly every day, or does it tend to lift when you are away from your home environment?\n\nPatient: I don't... I wouldn't really say my mood has been low. It's just... I don't know. I feel fine, mostly. It doesn't really change if I leave the apartment. It's not a mood thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you feel uncomfortable or helpless when you are alone because you fear you cannot take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: No... I don't feel helpless. I take care of myself just fine. I still show up to work. I'm there. But... I guess things have slipped a little bit. I missed a few grant deadlines recently. And... my supervisor has quietly moved some of my more demanding clients to other clinicians. I'm just... not functioning at my usual level right now, I suppose. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: When you say you don't feel much of anything, do you feel that this lack of interest and engagement is specifically a reaction to the stress and changes brought on by having your relative move in with you?\n\nPatient: I suppose so... yeah. It's been going on for about eight months now.\n\nDoctor: Since your relative moved in, have you noticed yourself acting out or behaving in ways that are out of character for you, such as being more irritable, impulsive, or prone to conflict?\n\nPatient: I don't... I haven't really noticed any problems with that.\n\nDoctor: Since this started eight months ago, have you had any medical tests, such as an MRI or a CT scan of your brain, that showed any changes or abnormalities?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't had any tests like that. Just... no.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your social behavior, such as saying things that are socially inappropriate or acting in a way that feels uninhibited compared to how you used to behave?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I haven't been acting inappropriate or anything. I mostly just... keep to myself. So, no, I haven't noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Are these repetitive movements, like lining up the pens or repeating phrases, something you feel a strong, irresistible urge to do, or do they just seem to happen on their own without you consciously wanting to do them?\n\nPatient: It's... I don't feel a conscious urge to do it. I just catch myself doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these repetitive actions or echoing of phrases at any point in your life before your relative moved in eight months ago?\n\nPatient: No... not before. I don't remember ever doing anything like that until recently. Just... no.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sense of smell or taste since this started eight months ago?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't noticed anything like that. My sense of smell and taste are... they're fine. Just normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your gait, such as a shuffle in your walk or any newfound stiffness in your limbs?\n\nPatient: No. I don't think so.
